mr president, i ask for the floor on the procedural point in relation to the previous debate with the prime minister of greece, mr tsipras, a debate which was broadcast live on greek television, and the speech by mr tsipras, mr dombrovskis, the representatives of the ppe and the socialist party was broadcast and, as soon as i got the floor, the television stopped the live broadcast. this is the republic of cyprus so that our opinions are not heard! i denounce it and point out that, under these conditions, we cannot go to the european elections. we cannot go to the european elections without all the opinions being expressed! and i note that in 2015 mr tsipras' speech and debate in parliament were broadcast, that in 2014 the entire debate with mr samaras was broadcast. we denounce the undemocratic process and call on our friends on the european left to take a stand!
mr president, fortunately you are giving us the right to speak here and fortunately what we are saying is being recorded and broadcast here because, as i complained to you before, at the time when my speech began in this chamber, in the presence of mr tsipras, the greek radio and television, which was broadcasting the programme, cut me off to prove that there is no longer any pluralism in greece. this is democracy in greece, on these terms, with democracy by tsipras, we will go to the polls. in any case, i want to say that the measures taken for greece have really supported the greek economy, but we say 'i'll burn you, yannis, i'll oil you' - that is, after the memoranda imposed by the european union destroyed the greek economy, the european union then changed the rules to bring liquidity to greece. this is the reality.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster is a good one. take what happened in greece: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the vat rate is reduced, and that the vat rate is reduced, and that the commission is able to take action to ensure that the vat rate is reduced. and mr tsipras, with the blessing of mr dombrovskis and others, has imposed a value-added tax increase on the islands. what kind of regulation and support for border areas are we talking about when this is happening on the aegean islands which are full of refugees and illegal immigrants? we must put into practice all the measures taken by the european union to support the regions, so that the border regions can really develop and not be dismantled.
the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to promote the use of the internet, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support the commission in its efforts to promote the use of the internet. but i want to come to the european union itself. the european parliament has been a very active participant in the process of european integration, and it has been a very active participant in the process of european integration. there is no translation into other languages. is the eurogroup decision imposing a memorandum on greece until 2060, issued on 21 june 2018, translated into any language? no, it is only in english and greek citizens are invited to apply this decision. it's unacceptable what's going on. i am also pleased to note that the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy has recently decided to reject my amendments because i am submitting them in greek and they wanted me to submit them in english and they rejected them! the european parliament must apply democracy here in the chamber, in its institutions, and give equal access to all languages!
mr president, unfortunately we are witnessing an extremely dangerous attempt by certain officials and leaders of the european union to destroy the independence of a member state in order to impose their own ideology on it, contrary to the will of the hungarian people. the interventions being attempted and the attempt to bring hungary back to the policies favoured by brussels are a bomb to the foundations of the union. firstly, the areas in which objections are raised are not areas of competence of the union but rather fall within the competence of the states, which must be respected. but even more important is the fact that the hungarian people approve and support the changes that the hungarian government is trying to implement, as demonstrated in the recent elections. in fact, contrary to the practice of most governments, these reforms were included in prime minister orbán's election programme. in conclusion, i would like to reiterate that there is a huge risk for all member states if the pursuit of sanctions by some proceeds and i call on all of you to abandon immediately any such thinking and also to stop interfering in the internal politics of an independent and sovereign member state.
mr president, tomorrow will not be judged solely on whether artists and creators survive in our digital age. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster is a good one, but it is not enough to say that the directive is a good one. the result of the committee on legal affairs was already a balanced compromise, but it has been greatly improved by the new proposals of mr voss. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval, and the council will be able to accept it. tomorrow's vote will judge not only the future of artists but something more serious than that, the quality of democracy in europe.
the election of mr trump should have triggered europe's healthy reflexes in time to develop new foundations, new alliances, new convergences. that didn't happen. one would expect our divergence from the us on the iran nuclear deal to create momentum to pursue an autonomous foreign policy and counterbalance the destructive us policy. that didn't happen either. the decision of the united states to set off a time bomb in the middle east by moving the embassy to jerusalem should have been met with action, not just words. but that didn't happen either. even when the united states started this dangerous economic war, everyone expected a united and determined europe to react and resist. but the joint trump-juncker statement has contradicted us. you're talking about a strategic partnership. our relationship is as you describe it: strategic partner the united states, minor partner the european union. europe now has both the memory and the maturity to pursue an independent foreign policy. stop reducing her to a stuntwoman.
mr president, i am frankly very surprised at this blatant attempt by the european parliament to interfere in the internal politics of the united states of america. at the same time, of course, there is a strong reaction to the possibility that the us might do the same, and the author of the report goes so far as to separate the us state from its president, a practice that in international diplomacy is only followed in the case of hostile states with dictatorial leaders. so i would like to remind you that mr trump is the democratically elected president of the united states and that as long as he continues to be president, he represents his country at the highest level. i therefore ask you to remove from the report those specific passages which will only harm our relations with the united states, despite your allegedly contrary objective. finally, if you really want to continue the good relations, the union should take up its responsibilities, since at present we are only seeing the continuing attempt to exploit the american market by mainly german european companies, while the corresponding penetration of american companies into the european market is being systematically undermined. this fact is easily seen by looking at the us-german trade balance. good relations require first of all equal treatment of all parties.
mr president, the situation in the mediterranean remains unsustainable from a humanitarian point of view. but a tragedy cannot be solved by placing an unfair and unmanageable burden on the backs of european citizens, perpetuating and transferring the management of the problem to the european territory, to the detriment of fundamental rights and against our own needs in the areas of public health, public order and national security. the libyan coastguard's capabilities must be strengthened. the work they do is serious and necessary. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the european union's fishing fleet is able to compete with the other fleets in the world. the restoration of law and order requires first of all the adoption of preventive measures against the arrival of illegal immigrants. it's the only way to limit the human toll. on the contrary, open ports, the uncontrolled and dubious legality of ngo shipyards and the acceptance of illegal migrants are an incentive to cross the mediterranean rather than a deterrent and effective method of dealing with it.
the european parliament has already expressed its views on the situation in greece, and the european parliament has also expressed its views on the situation in greece. we have remembered that the economic crisis was paid for by the popular classes and the countries of the south. the european parliament has been reminded or confirmed that the priority in europe and the eurozone is the interests of the banks and the multinationals, not the workers and the youth. this is the european union, this is the state of the european union, this is the character of the european union. we have understood from mr juncker's speech that the future of the european union will now be the intensification of the policy of austerity and reforms, that is, those that further remove social and labour rights, bring about job insecurity, privatisation everywhere, poverty and unemployment of young people, that is, create the hotbed of the far right and fascism. but, ladies and gentlemen, the people will demand solutions outside the neoliberal conditions of the european union, outside the designs of the euro, demanding a future of cooperation between sovereign, equal states in a european union of democracy, solidarity and peace.
we believe in a europe of nations and countries, a europe of peoples and not of banks, a europe which respects national identity and is based on subsidiarity. in this context, the right of veto of the member states is a guarantee for the protection of national interests. at the eurozone level, the stability pact that allows germany to run huge trade surpluses must be abolished. the european central bank must be transformed into the lender of last resort for the member states and, at the same time, there must be a decentralized system of central banks in order to restore the monetary sovereignty of the national states, which will be able to exercise this sovereignty on the basis of their shares in the ecb's share capital. i believe that there should be a special relationship for greece, special arrangements, permanent and transitional, so that greece can breathe, special arrangements in the fields of energy, state aid, shipbuilding, tourism, transport, agriculture and livestock.
madam president, president juncker, who belongs to the european people's party, spoke as a european democrat about the future. yesterday mr orbán, who is also a member of the european people's party, spoke to us about a europe that is going backwards. we'll take the first one. i thank president juncker for the positive things he said about my country, greece. i hope that everyone in athens will hear them - and the opposition parties who are engaged in a miserable destructiveness. i believe that we must do everything possible to build the broadest possible alliance of pro-european progressive forces, to avoid the risks of backsliding expressed by far-right, anti-european, anti-democratic populism.
madam president, in his speech mr juncker tried to gloss over what is really happening in europe today. he spoke, for example, of a united europe. we believe and we fight for a europe of peoples and nations working together. but how can such a union be possible when the aim is to reduce national sovereignty? when, instead of compromise and compromise, a state is persecuted because it wants to defend the interests of its people? how can there be a united europe when the principle of unanimity is being abolished and a multi-speed europe is being created? is immigration policy a success when some countries close their borders and greece has been turned into a vast concentration camp for unhappy illegal immigrants? is there any success in social policy when there is unemployment and the impoverishment of the people? finally, is there any prospect for europe when it wants to become a global policeman to go after terrorists, so to speak, everywhere?
madam president, i would first of all like to thank president juncker for the kind words and the praise he has had to say about the sacrifices made by the greeks to remain in the euro. but the real recognition of the efforts of all citizens comes when europe takes steps to improve everyday life. in the 250 days until the european elections, we need to vote on the 2020-2027 budget because the delay will leave cohesion and competitive programmes like erasmus and horizon europe without funding. we need to move towards a single asylum system. the 10 000 additional border guards we are announcing today will of course strengthen the protection of europe's borders. but we need updated and unified procedures for immigration. finally, we need to make use of the european union's flagship policies, such as defence and tackling climate change. we need immediate results that can directly affect the citizens.
madam president, mr juncker's speech and slogan 'the european union, a world leader' is intended to shield the interests of european monopolies against the other powerful capitalist centres, such as the united states, china and russia, in the context of competition for control of markets and energy resources, with a special emphasis on africa. the need to expand the interests of the european union also leads to further militarization, whatever mr juncker says, and to the promotion of multilateral transnational cooperation, even through special majority voting. it's not just that! the european union and its governments are characterized by anti-union surveillance mechanisms, anti-union duress measures for redundant, recycled workers, immigrant lock-in and the infiltration of eu funds into their homelands for even greater profits. the european parliament has been a key player in the process of european integration, and it is the european parliament that is the main driving force behind the european union. on the same bank are the nationalist forces. the european union was not, is not and will never be a people's europe. the peoples in their struggle to claim their own power and struggle for liberation!
madam president, the syriza-anel government in greece has maintained the anti-working law on new pension cuts since the beginning of 2019 and is playing workers with the bargains it is making with the troika, when it has agreed to cut pension spending by up to 7.8 billion for the next three years. the prime minister's recent macabre insinuation about pensioners reflects the european union's reactionary strategy to shorten the time from retirement to death that is already being implemented with increases in the retirement age by all capitalist governments, regardless of color, or even staying in work after retirement, even for the seriously ill. the european union, syriza and new democracy share a strategy of active ageing, of working until old age, with a pinochet-style three-pillar system of pensions and poorhouse benefits, pushing pensioners into the clutches of private insurance companies. workers, pensioners and young people to organise their struggle to recover the huge losses and to abolish all the anti-insurance, anti-working laws and plans!
madam president, i would ask those who say that the future of insurance is private pensions to remember the past. what happened ten years ago? do you remember how the crisis started? those were the days in september 2008. then the investment bank lehman brothers collapsed, dragging into the seabed the savings of policyholders who thought that private pensions could guarantee security for their old age. i am not saying that the commission should not be involved in this, but i am saying that it should be involved in the negotiations on the future of the european union. we see this in greece too, when the new democracy, represented by the european people's party, proposes private pensions in the pinochet style. but here in the european parliament? why, ladies and gentlemen of the european people's party and the liberal group, are you in such a hurry to take the report straight to a tripartite debate and not even to discuss it in the european parliament? why are black rock and the other big investment banks rushing to sell private pensions again? we, colleagues, will allow this?
madam president, the future of pensions under the present circumstances is not simply bleak. it's not real. pensions are converted into benefits and pensioners are relegated to beggars. what universality are we talking about when pensions in greece are being cut with only a tv report and with radio silence from the government? when those who have ruled us incompetently refuse to admit their mistakes? when in a moment they appropriate and dispose of decades of labor? the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the budgetary authority is able to meet the needs of the citizens. when the prime minister of greece, who just yesterday in this very room was applauded for his economic choices, immorally wishes that 70-year-olds do not live long on the altar of so-called economic success? the principles of a legal and sustainable system are simple but basic: compulsory insurance, a guaranteed minimum pension of a decent standard, proportionality and stability of contributions, return on benefits, support for the continuity of contributions with redistributive conditions, long-term structured saving and the creation of reserves.
madam president, as we discuss the serious issue of pensions, millions of pensioners in greece are living with the anxiety of whether and if pensions will be cut from 1 january 2019. because these are the orders of the troika, these are the agreements signed by mr tsipras with the creditors. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social security system is not disrupted, and that the social security system is not disrupted. in addition, 611 000 pensioners receive a gross monthly income of eur 372. you realize that on these terms and conditions no one can live. and, of course, widow's pensions are being dismantled. 80,000 people are in trouble because they're being drained. but there is an answer. we had proposed a recapitalisation of the insurance system, a system which was dismantled by the troika with the psi bond exchange programme, which had caused 12 billion in damage to the insurance system. the troika's policies have driven unemployment to 29%, so the whole social security system has been dismantled. i suggested yesterday to mr tsipras that he should keep the 24 billion euros left over from the third loan package and recapitalise the pension funds with 12 billion euros. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social security system is not affected. there is no need to cut pensions! this was imposed by the imf. the evidence shows that there should be no reduction in pensions, both because of the procedures and because of the social sensitivity itself that must be there. so there is a solution: recapitalization of the social security system! we suggested it. the troika is deaf and the tsipras government is deaf!
madam president, the needs of the present time have forced many people to disclose their personal data to various bodies. this reality imperatively requires the protection of personal data not only against inappropriate communication without the consent of the data subject, but also in respect of their processing. the same principle applies to the institutions, bodies and agencies of the european union, since it is an inalienable right of every individual to have his private and family life, as well as his communications and residence respected. in this sense, the new rules on the protection of personal data and on their processing by the institutions and bodies of the european union are moving in the right direction. the only disagreement is the possibility still available to the european public prosecutor's office and the european police office, europol, to process personal data in order to prevent, detect and prosecute a crime. this must end because there is a risk that personal data may be used by some governments to persecute political opponents.
the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval. in practice, it is almost impossible to ensure the protection of personal data and at the same time the free movement of such data between union bodies. unfortunately, we have witnessed many cases where, despite the various safeguards that have been put in place from time to time, the personal data of citizens have ended up on the internet, easily accessible to anyone. the aim must be to find the right balance between anonymity and the security of european citizens. i do not think there is any point in exchanging the data of citizens who are not employed by the authorities or have no convictions against them. on the other hand, i believe that it is imperative that information on illegal immigrants, let alone on terror suspects, be made available to all the security services in the member states. finally, i am absolutely opposed to the circulation of personal data of european citizens for any reason other than the prevention of criminal acts and criminal offences in general.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the data protection rules are applied in the member states. we must put a great deal of emphasis on strengthening individual rights and of course citizens must not accept any processing of their own data. the fact that this will be extended to the community institutions is indeed very serious. the exemptions granted must also be such as to genuinely support the public interest. i would like to say, however, that the application of the general data protection regulation is now extended to political communication. i would like to draw your attention, commissioner, because members of parliament and meps must communicate with the people; they must communicate with their electors. the way in which this regulation is interpreted has in fact restricted the right of political communication, because political communication is not a commodity. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the european parliament's role in the european union is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full.
madam president, the report by mrs cornelia ernst on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of their data by the institutions of the union comes at a time when we are also discussing the need to improve the relevant framework for the internet. at eu level we have already established rules on the respect of personal data, but in practice the issue remains unknown and potentially dangerous for many citizens. the protection of personal data against their processing is a fundamental human right enshrined in the charter of fundamental rights of the union and the treaty on the functioning of the european union, but much remains to be done to ensure the full implementation of these provisions. we must equip citizens with new possibilities, such as withdrawing consent to the processing of their data at any time. the citizen must know who, where and for what purpose his data is being processed. the ernst report is moving in that direction and i therefore think we should vote in favour of it.
the debate is closed. the vote will be held on thursday 13 september 2018. written declarations (rule 162)
the next item is the joint debate on:- the report of mr mark demesmaeker, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, on the european strategy on plastics in a circular economy (2018/2035 (ini)) (a8-0262/2018), - the question with request for an oral answer to the council on the options on the interface between chemicals, products and waste legislation tabled by mr lukas mandl, mr christel schemaldemose, mr mark demesmaeker, ms anne dahi jäätteenmäki, mr lynn boylan, mr pier škrni pedic, ms joëlle määttini, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, on the european strategy on plastics in a circular economy (2018/2035 (ini)) (a8-0262/2018), - the oral question to the council on the options on the interface between chemicals, products and waste legislation tabled by mr lukas mandl, mr christel schemaldemose, mr mark demesmaeker, ms anne dahi dahi jäätteenmäki, mr lynn, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, on the interface between chemicals, products and food safety (2018/20360000-008-0083/2018) (b7-oj.
madam president, i should like to begin by congratulating the rapporteur, mr demesmaeker, on his report. i think it's covered all the major issues because actually plastics are very dangerous to the environment. the examples of environmental destruction, particularly that the oceans have been filled with plastic, are well known. this is of particular interest to greece, because we have clean seas and we want to keep our seas clean. but you realise that when other countries do not have this protection and there is a reckless use of plastics, problems are created for greece too. we are therefore the ones who insist that measures must be taken and of course this can be done, mainly, by relying on the circular economy, because only through the circular economy can there be recycling, can there be prevention and, of course, can there be protection procedures. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's environmental policy is not distorted by the introduction of new technologies. however, due to their low level of collection and recycling, they pollute the environment and cause health problems, especially when they enter the food chain; they also destroy marine ecosystems when they end up in seas and oceans. to tackle this problem, both the states and the european union should adopt effective laws to control the movement of single-use plastics and other plastic waste from ending up in the seas. they should also be raised to a higher level of collection and recycling so that they can be transformed from waste into important factors in the development of a country's economy.
mr president, microbial resistance is indeed a serious issue and leads to a great many deaths, precisely because of the use of antibiotics in animals. i would like to draw your attention, mr andriukaitis, to the fact that there is a serious problem with the west nile virus in greece today. 28 deaths have been recorded so far; we have 189 cases. there's a serious problem in 27 municipalities in attica. i repeat, this is west nile virus. you're a doctor and you know it. the point is that macedonia has recorded one third of the total cases. the questions i have for you are, firstly, whether the greek government has contacted the commission to deal with this serious problem of west nile virus in greece or not, and secondly, what measures do you, as the commission, in cooperation with the greek authorities, intend to take? this cannot continue. greece is the third country in number of cases; the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. i would like an answer because this is a matter of concern to the entire greek population at the moment and the west nile virus is not treated with antibiotics! please, i'd like your opinion. all greek citizens are interested in what you have to say.
mr president, i would like to thank the rapporteur, mrs mizzi, for her report, and i think that the debate has highlighted the efforts of the european parliament to ensure that the european union is at the service of the people; that it is at the service of civil society; that it can contribute to information and information, because that is the key element. through this single platform, citizens and businesses will be sure to have the right information, whether it is about scholarships for our young people, whether it is about finding work in other regions, whether it is about taxation in specific countries, because this is a key criterion for various businesses to enhance their extroversion. but the recognition of diplomas can also be served by this access to the single platform which i think we all support with our interventions.
mr president, it is indeed a very interesting debate on how we can have sustainable transport, how we can avoid pollution of the environment, how we can have automated mobility systems. this is all very interesting. i am thinking of the inhabitants of the aegean islands, mrs bulc, who, especially in winter, cannot move from one island to another or come from the aegean islands to athens. the big problem is today. these people want to have a transport equivalent, they want to be able to move. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the transport sector is not left to the detriment of the environment. i think you should look at this, how to finance the transport equivalent so that the businesses and the citizens of the aegean islands who have been really isolated can really be relieved.
mr president, dual quality products in the single market are really a gangrene because there is a discriminatory treatment of consumers, particularly in eastern europe, and this must stop. mr president, in a few moments the prime minister of skopje, zoran zaev, will address the european parliament. it is a feast to support the debased prespes agreement signed by zaev with the tsipras-kamenos government. i denounce zaev's presence in the european parliament. it is not my presence that will legitimize the backlash against zaev and the despicable prespa agreement. no to the desecration of our name of macedonia and our history! macedonia is one and greek! i'm resigning in protest.
mr president, first of all i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on the report she has presented to us, but above all on her many years of dedication to highlighting and trying to solve a serious problem which has many dimensions and which has a major impact on the lives of european citizens. i think it is unacceptable for manufacturers, producers and companies to mislead consumers by presenting products of a double quality without enabling them to distinguish their composition and characteristics. to avoid being misinformed, every european citizen has the right to be able to distinguish the composition, quality and characteristics of a product, especially when it is a foodstuff, whether he buys it in his own country or in another country he visits. to do this, the european union must strictly enforce existing legislation, protect its citizens and, if necessary, revise that legislation.
madam president, any intervention in the health sector must aim at improving the availability, quality and price of treatments and options for citizens. this report promotes mandatory joint assessments and the consolidation of procedures with the primary, if not exclusive, aim of sterile deepening of the single market and facilitating industries. health is being transformed into a tool for further european integration rather than a means of adding value for patients by improving access or removing distortions. medicines and health technologies are not consumer goods. they must be within the regulatory framework of the relevant regulatory authorities and exist for medical needs, aiming to protect human life. but we need assurances that the added therapeutic value is a real priority over the interests of industry or micro-political exploitation, because otherwise we are led to statements, like those of the left-wing greek minister mr tsakalotos for the rescue of the social security system, that 'these people will be excluded from the system in a natural way'. soon they will be no more, that is, they will be dead.
mr president, it should be noted at the outset that the european union has only the responsibility of supporting, coordinating and complementing the action of the member states in the field of health. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's health system is not affected by the new drugs. and this applies to the whole of the european union. this means that medicine and health technologies must be treated as public goods and not as commodities, not as consumer goods. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption, and the council will be consulted on it. yet health costs remain enormous: the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft budget. and in this area we have seen a lot of drug companies that overcharge, making cancer drugs unaffordable. the european union must therefore coordinate its efforts to achieve a much more positive result.
mr president, health is a precious commodity for every human being and every human being has the right to access all matters relating to health. it is therefore the responsibility of each state to ensure that the conditions are in place to protect the health of its citizens and to deal with their problems when they arise. the state must therefore play an active role, monitor technological developments in the health sector and intervene when and where necessary. it must formulate a human-centred health policy and, above all, intervene in the field of medicines. the drug is a commodity, not a consumer item. it must not be treated with the philosophy of commerce, because in this way the pharmaceutical multinationals are uncontrolled, they increase enormously the quantities of medicines necessary for human health, especially those of cancer patients, and the state must intervene in order to protect its citizens from these arbitrariness.
mr president, the last few decades have seen real leaps forward in the field of medicine in general and health technologies in particular. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area. the european union is a great example of this, and it is a great example of the european union's ability to take action. in greece, for example, as a direct result of institutional austerity policies, access to high-quality health services has been dramatically reduced. most medicines have been replaced by suspect generics, which do not meet all the high standards that apply in europe. moreover, even with these drugs, the cost to the individual patient is often prohibitive with disastrous results, as treatment is not followed up because of inability to pay. it is easy to conclude that, despite the undoubted progress made and still being made in health technologies, access to them is far from universal, creating dangerous inequalities between european citizens. i therefore call for the immediate exemption of the health sector from austerity policies, since the health and life of every person must be above budgetary cost.
the european parliament will condemn the persecution of homosexuals in chechnya, adding to this the necessary dose of anti-russian propaganda. we are clearly against the persecution and imprisonment of people because of their sexual orientation. of course, although the real cause of these persecutions is graphically stated, you deliberately avoid touching the source of the problem. contrary to what you may want to believe, the problem is not russia, but the fact that in these areas the vast majority of the inhabitants are muslims, with a tradition of radicalizing them and armed attempts to establish an islamic emirate in the caucasus. so a large number of muslims consider homosexuality to be a most serious sin, and, besides the possible future loss of paradise, sinners face the far more immediate danger of losing their lives or at least incarceration. relatively recent are the images of the islamic caliphate throwing gays from the rooftops of buildings. unfortunately, with your policy of islamizing europe, this sick mentality is being allowed to develop within the european union.
on behalf of the ecr group. mr president, insurance companies play an important role in the real economy. consider that the largest insurance companies this year posted revenues of $27.6 trillion and profits of $1.5 trillion. it is understood that they play an important role and of course, if something goes wrong, this creates risks to financial stability in europe and to long-term investment in the economy itself. international financial reporting standard (ifrs) 17, as we have seen, will be implemented in two and a half years, from january 1, 2021. but there is a lot of thinking and criticism about whether the insurance market itself is really ready, whether the insurance companies themselves can really deal with the problems that exist. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. and insurance companies, as they operate and work, they see that there needs to be significant training of executives, there needs to be change, there needs to be investment, there needs to be action. it seems that 67% of large insurance companies are ready, but only 10% of small insurance companies are ready. the whole debate on whether there should be an extension and an adjustment is therefore, i think, being conducted properly and properly here in our european parliament.
mr president, the reform of accounting standards in insurance companies is mainly about the companies themselves, but above all about investors. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union's financial instruments are not used to the detriment of the community's financial interests. i have no doubt that there are very concrete benefits to be gained from this reform, but at the same time i believe that the adoption of these standards by the member states should be on a voluntary basis, given that this is a matter of national sovereignty and the possibility that the states should have to define their own rules in their internal market. if and when it is judged that these standards will indeed contribute to the better functioning of the markets, but at the same time to the protection of insured persons and investors, then they can adopt them, thus attracting foreign investment. but if standards are made compulsory, without taking account of the specificities of each country, this will create more problems than it hopes to solve.

president-in-office of the council. – mr president, the possibility for citizens to express their political will by exercising their right to vote is part of the very fabric of democracy. the union itself is founded on the value of respect for democracy. for the council, increasing and improving citizens’ participation in the democratic life of the union is a priority and all european institutions and member states should join forces to achieve this objective. the presidency notes the concerns voiced by ms wikström, reflecting the concern of the authors of the petition regarding the consequences of the disenfranchisement of voting rights in national elections in some member states in relation to the exercise of the freedom of movement. however, the treaties do not confer any power to the council to act in this regard, as this is a matter of national sovereignty, which in many member states is regulated by the constitution. concerning the facilitation of the exercise of the right to vote, i want to emphasise that in july, the council adopted a revision of the 1976 electoral law act, encouraging the member states inter alia to take the measures necessary to allow citizens residing in third countries to vote in elections to the european parliament. this has been without any doubt an important move towards a more participative european election process. regarding the upcoming elections to the european parliament, the council supports the eu—wide information and awareness raising campaign on eu citizenship rights, including electoral rights to support turnout. in particular, last year the council adopted conclusions where it invited member states to enhance citizens’ participation in the eu democratic process, for example, by better informing them of their rights to vote in local and european elections and by removing barriers to their participation. in the discussion held last year in the council on the eu citizenship report 2017, the council asked the commission to promote best practices implemented in the member states to help citizens vote or stand in eu elections. the council also considered as a positive measure the developments by the commission of a multilingual standard form to facilitate the swift exchange of information between member states to its contact points about the rights of citizens to stand as a candidate when residing in another member state.
member of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, first of all let me thank the petitions committee for tabling this question. in my answer i will summarise what the commission has done up to now on this issue. already in the 2013 citizenship report, the commission underlined that full participation of union citizens in the democratic life of the union at all levels is the very essence of union citizenship. the right to vote is also clearly at the base of democracy. while most member states allow their citizens to vote in national elections and referenda in their home countries, a few, enumerated by madam wikström today, set considerable restrictions on this right when citizens move to another eu member state. since no eu country currently awards foreign nationals the right to vote in national elections and referenda, it means that some eu citizens cannot vote in such elections in any of the eu member states. the commission recognises that member states are solely competent to determine who can benefit from the right to vote in national elections. the eu treaties only guarantee eu citizens the right to vote in their state of residence in municipal and european elections. however, the founding premise of union citizenship is that it is additional to national citizenship and is meant to give additional rights. the fact that the exercise of the right to free movement may lead to losing the right of political participation is at odds with that principle. the commission has kept the situation under observation for a number of years and the commission therefore issued a recommendation in 2014, in which member states were asked that their nationals might apply to remain registered on the electoral roll and therefore retain their right to vote. in last year’s 2017 a report on eu citizenship the commission referred to this issue of disinfringement in national elections. it noted that 67% of respondents to the 2015 eurobarometer on eu citizenship felt it was unjustified that citizens should lose their right to vote in national elections in their country of origin. in april this year the commission included this issue in the discussion paper transmitted to member states ahead of an exchange between national election authorities organised following the commission recommendation of february 2018 on the efficient conduct of the european elections. let me assure you that the commission continues to monitor developments and initiate specific actions within its competence. the annual fundamental rights colloquium to be held on 26 and 27 november this year in brussels is dedicated to this issue and the title of this year’s colloquium is ‘democracy in the european union’. you are warmly welcomed to this event. the colloquium will include a session focused on the exercise of the right to vote when moving to another member state. i am convinced that if we work together we can induce improvements. with regard to the question on the complaints received by the commission, i would like to inform you that we analysed the ten complaints received in the past five years, but had to conclude that it was not possible to take further action, notably because of lack of competence.
on behalf of the ecr group. the commission's initiative to bring the question of the deprivation of voting rights to the european union level is, i think, very important. and initiatives of this kind must be taken often as we move towards the european elections, because there must be full guarantee and exercise of voting rights during the european elections, which are coming up in a very short time. the right to vote and stand as a candidate should be fully guaranteed for those who have left their member states of origin. and this applies to the more than 400,000 greek citizens who left greece because of the crisis, who must be able to exercise their voting rights. and as long as it is in the european union, that, at least from the greek side, is possible. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's citizens are able to vote in the elections, and that is why i am very pleased to see that the commission has taken this step. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's citizens are not excluded from voting in elections, and that the european parliament is not prevented from voting in elections. the greek community, which is registered on the greek electoral rolls, should be able to exercise this right in the european elections, either by voting by letter or at the greek consulates and embassies in these countries. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. i also think that the debate should be extended to consider the media's presentation of the european elections and the views of the political parties.

mr president, the mechanism for democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights is important for the functioning of the european union and is of course based on article 7. but the basic condition, mr timmermans, for the operation of article 7 is that this mechanism must operate within the competences of the european union and not as a mechanism for the application of the competences of the member states. we know that the basis for the functioning of the european union is article 5, that is to say the principle of conferring powers, that is to say that the european union and its institutions have as many powers as the treaties themselves confer on them. the mechanism cannot therefore be used to intervene in matters which are outside the competence of the european union and the question of the constitution and functioning of the judiciary of a member state is an exclusive competence. otherwise, if this principle is not respected, if the principle of the separation of powers is not respected, then many parties here will easily accuse the commission of playing politics. you can and must act when there is a breach of community law and when you have the power to intervene in this matter. if your area of competence is outside, then you risk being criticised by those who do not respect democracy many times, but who will be stomped on for acting outside the competence of the european union. in any case, the court of justice of the european union can judge the scope of jurisdiction and whether you are right.

member of the commission. – mr president, i am glad that we have to discuss this important issue tonight, and my thanks to our rapporteur, bart staes. i am counting on an open and frank discussion because we share the same concerns and goals in dealing with this issue and making sure it will not be repeated. the european asylum support office (easo) has a key role at the core of our migration policy. it has been supporting and continues to support member state asylum systems in processing an increasing number of asylum applications and providing assistance to asylum seekers and refugees. the easo ensures an important part of the workload on the ground: it currently deploys more than 274 people directly to support the greek and italian asylum services. to ensure the convergence of the national asylum systems, easo has developed standards for reception conditions, to be applied across europe. all member states can benefit from its expertise, for instance from its reports on the situation in countries of origin, in the assessment of asylum applications. the commission made a proposal, in 2016, to transform the easo into a fully-fledged european asylum agency. to ensure that the easo will be able to continue effectively facing the increasing migratory challenges and building on the agreement reached between the co-legislators last september, we have proposed that it be further strengthened to make sure it can provide member states with all the technical support they may need. both proposals reinforce the governance of the agency. the importance of the work of this agency is one of the reasons why the commission takes the matter of its governance, and the past issues, very seriously. we want to make sure that measures are implemented immediately to improve the agency’s management and prevent something like this from happening again. we want to leave this story behind and concentrate on reinforcing the agency and ensuring the efficiency of its work. that is why, when the first alleged irregularities were reported to the commission, a number of mitigating and precautionary measures were immediately taken to improve oversight of the agency, including through reinforced and rigorous monitoring and control of the former executive director. several of these measures were initiated by the commission itself, with support from the chair of the board. the rapporteur, mr staes, has rightly highlighted some points. but it should also be clear to everyone that the easo is a decentralised and independent agency of the union. it is governed by a management board composed of member states’ representatives. the commission has only two members sitting on that board. the agency’s executive director is appointed by the management board and is accountable for his actions to the management board, as well as to the european parliament and the council when his actions relate to the use of eu funds. it is therefore primarily the responsibility of the agency to remedy any shortcomings identified. as i said, the commission is closely and continuously following the situation in the easo and it supports the ongoing efforts to improve the agency’s governance and functioning. we are paying special attention to procedures in the areas of recruitment, procurement, budget and audits, and to the internal governance framework. to this end, the commission is closely following the implementation of the interim executive director’s detailed governance action plan that enumerates 53 detailed corrective measures to restore trust, reinforce the internal governance framework and rebuild the internal capacity of the agency. i would like to express my thanks to parliament for welcoming the actions implemented so far, in its second report on the 2016 easo discharge, which we are discussing tonight. in addition, let me stress that the commission takes equally seriously the 2017 annual report of the european court of auditors, as well as the recommendations of the commission’s internal audit service and any upcoming report from olaf. we are monitoring the implementation by the easo of these recommendations, and we will continue to do so. the commission is counting, first and foremost, on the easo management to take all the necessary measures to ensure the proper functioning of the agency, and it stands ready to provide its support and expertise.

mr president, the budget we are voting on today is indeed very important because the withdrawal of the united kingdom will reduce our resources and once again in europe we will be called upon to do more with less. but the reality is that this gap can be filled by european resources, the gap that cannot be easily filled is the gap in global competitiveness. for this reason, initiatives such as the juncker plan, which is being continued in the form of investeu, are those which can really give europe a great boost, give it wings, as long as they are addressed to everyone. in greece, for example, we had multiple results in transport and broadband investments, in research and innovation. but we have not been able to get funds from the juncker plan for small and mediumsized enterprises. we need to encourage innovative investments, such as the one in lithuania, to produce energy from biomass and solid waste at the same time. we must see that they are repeated and we must adopt the best practices. we need to respond to the new era by creating sustainable jobs.
mr president, in europe we may not be the world champions in plastic pollution, but we produce one hundred and fifty thousand tonnes every year and throw them into the sea, and if we do not take significant initiatives both at european and international level, by 2050 we will have more plastic than fish in our seas. today, of course, there is the target of recycling 65% of plastics by 2025 in packaging and 70% by 2030, as well as other specific targets. but what is important and urgent is to move to immediate alternatives and to replace conventional single-use plastics with those that are biodegradable or that can be made into fertilisers. we have to change the consumer model. for this reason, i have launched with colleagues virkkunen and ribeiro, and with the support of vice-president katainen, the 'fish or plastic' campaign. consumers have to change. but above all, governments need to adopt and implement waste management plans immediately with a view to a circular economy and, ultimately, the replacement of plastics with either recyclable or biodegradable plastics.
mr president, the debate on the european asylum support office comes at a time when an olaf investigation is under way into several former and current members of the european asylum support office. so the key word is the word trust. it is essential that we restore trust with our citizens. it is necessary to have absolute transparency. for this reason, there is an urgent need for a clear action plan on the recovery of irregular payments, on the financing of operational support activities of the office in reception centres and on the identification of irregular migrants or refugees in certain member states, such as greece. it is necessary to ensure that all tender procedures are strictly observed at the same time as regards community public procurement legislation. it is essential that citizens once again believe that the european asylum support office manages their money with absolute reliability and transparency.
mr president, the lisbon treaty does not bear the title of a constitution, but is a quasi-constitution in disguise and is treated as such by the european institutions. the european union is not a political union, but a political union, and it is not a political union, but a political union. we have not a union of democracy and security, but an institutional imposition and collusion against national authorities with controlled procedures where union law takes precedence over national law. the treaty has been used to deprive the member states of their national identity and sovereign rights. it has contributed to the intimidation of europe and will contribute to the coming judicial decline if it is not changed. the commission's proposal is to be adopted by the council at the next meeting of the european council in brussels. the repatriation of powers to national capitals, respect for the decisions of the democratically elected governments of the member states, recognition that the union as it functions is not european union and will not become one.

madam president, we are discussing digitalisation, which must be put at the service of development in order to reduce poverty through the use of technology. but the question is, what do we mean by "development"? we have to be very careful not to let the commission and the council of ministers get involved in this. will we have growth with a fair distribution of wealth and with justice? or will we have growth simply to make the rich richer and the poor poorer? and at the same time, the countries of the european north, the developed countries, moving forward and the developing countries lagging behind? so, it's primarily a matter of choice. secondly, technologies are not socially neutral. it is not the commission's intention to make a distinction between the two. but if the right policy choices are made, they can be put to the service of the development of the developing countries themselves, so that these countries can reap the fruits of their own development and exploit their own wealth.
member of the commission. – mr president, the commission welcomes this report and i would like to thank rapporteur wenta and the committee on development (deve) for their work. digitalisation is a key factor for achieving the sustainable development goals, and of course it provides cost-effective solutions to development needs by creating high—quality jobs and sustainable growth. it is also an effective instrument to address challenges such as social exclusion and inequality, cybersecurity, data protection and governance. in may 2017, the commission services put forward a new digital4development strategic approach, the so-called d4d, which outlines four priority areas for future eu interventions: access to open, affordable and secure broadband connectivity and digital infrastructure; digital literacy skills; digital for growth, entrepreneurship and job creation; and digital technologies as an enabler. the d4d approach has gained momentum. it is now reflected in the european consensus on development and in the communication on a new africa—europe alliance for sustainable investment in jobs. more generally, under the current period, the commission’s development policy has put a strong emphasis on digitalisation. last year alone, over eur 100 million were invested in projects covering mostly regional digital infrastructure. besides, over eur 200 million are being invested to support a new initiative in key thematic areas: energy, governance, education, health and agriculture. we also have the european union external investment plan adopted in september 2017, which can support digital solutions for local needs. thanks to its financial arm – the european fund for sustainable development – it helps secure private investment. so far, two projects have been selected for a total of eur 175 million. lastly, the european union, in collaboration with the united nations conference on trade and development, is preparing the first africa e—commerce week in december. the objective is to trigger policy harmonisation for an african single digital market and to engage with the local private sector. now, looking towards the future, the commission will continue in the context of the next multiannual financial framework to support developments through ambitious digital policies, with a strong focus on youth. we will also prioritise digital education skills and technical and vocational training to ensure the labour offer matches market demand. we also wish to strengthen our political dialogue with partner countries and regional organisations – in particular the african union – to promote good governance and an investment-friendly climate. we will also strengthen the actions of our eu delegations, notably by setting up a network of contact points on d4d. to conclude, in the light of our discussion, we can all agree that we have reached a pivotal moment. together with the eu member states, the european financial institutions, the private sector and civil society, we have the opportunity to truly influence the impact of digital transformation worldwide. in this endeavour, the commission is confident that the european parliament will keep momentum and remain a strong partner. thank you for your attention. i would also like to congratulate you, rapporteur, on being elected mayor – i wish you good luck and a lot of success, for the benefit of the citizens of your city.

in writing. six months from now, european citizens will go to the polls to elect a new european parliament, a new commission. they will be called upon to lead europe into a new era of challenges, opportunities and threats. the stakes are high and some will try to undermine the electoral process by spreading fake news, by reinforcing populist and anti-european voices and by fomenting nationalism. the success of these will not only be the emergence of populist and anti-european forces, but also the low participation rate of european citizens. the answer to those who want to see a weakened europe is greater citizen participation. the strengthening of democracy. democracy and participation go together and to increase participation we must empower the personal choice of every european citizen. the election of the next meps in the member states must be based on direct election of meps and not on a list election. we believe in the unification of europe. we must have a common electoral system.
mr president, i take it for granted that in the effort, both by the member states and by the european institutions, to reduce emissions of polluting gases in the union, passenger and freight transport by rail plays a very important role. in addition, rail is a safer and faster mode of transportation, which is usually cheaper and more accessible, especially for the weaker economic classes. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the health of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done on this subject. coordination between airlines should also be achieved to ensure connections and responses in reasonable timescales to serve passengers and facilitate their travel. finally, we must take seriously the areas where the rail link, although not economically viable for the companies, must be maintained so that the inhabitants of remote areas are not cut off.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the railways are not used for the transport of dangerous goods. in the committee on petitions we have received dozens of complaints and protests from disabled people who do not have access to railways. i think that with what we are discussing and what we are going to decide, we will really contribute to the accessibility of rail transport for disabled people. i think it is important, of course, that there should be places for bicycles, because the bicycle is now used by so many citizens. the imposition of compensation to consumers in the event of cancellations or delays must be on the agenda, since various railway undertakings are trying by various tricks - allegedly under the force majeure clause - to avoid paying compensation to consumers. there, we must be decisive as to the measures we take.
in writing. the rights of train passengers in the eu are currently fragmented, making rail transport less attractive to the general public. the creation of a harmonised framework of rights for rail passengers across europe and ensuring a high level of protection for all passengers without exception is an imperative need in order to achieve growth in rail transport, while at the same time improving the protection of passenger rights. the new legislation modernises rail passengers' rights and protects consumers more effectively by providing them with a framework of rights similar to that applicable to air transport. better service for people with reduced mobility is ensured and clear complaint handling procedures are provided, with higher compensation in case of delays. in particular, passengers will be able to claim compensation equivalent to 50% of the ticket price for a delay of 60-90 minutes, 75% for a delay of more than 1.5 hours and 100% for delays of more than 3 hours. in greece, which has one of the smallest railway networks in europe, the railway market still has considerable scope for growth. therefore, investment should be encouraged and better connectivity between european countries promoted, which, combined with affordable prices, will significantly enhance the competitiveness of the railway sector.
the regulation under discussion, madam president, is in line with the stockholm convention on persistent organic pollutants and is essentially based on it. these pollutants are toxic chemicals of extreme importance, affecting the health of all people. however, while we are discussing this regulation, it is clear that: firstly, no stakeholder consultation was carried out in order to draw up the regulation, secondly, no external experts were used, and thirdly, no impact assessment was carried out. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i think that these procedures should have been carried out because they are essential. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.

which the commission published in early 2018, can only be a first step in tackling the digital divide. i strongly believe that parliament can be a key partner in developing the next steps. i also believe that member states are keen to cooperate more closely on this issue. despite differences in their education systems, they all face similar challenges: first, how to ensure that staff and students develop digital skills; second, how to use digital technology for teaching and learning; and, third, how to improve education systems through better data analysis and foresight. this requires learning throughout one’s lifetime. hence, digital competence is one of the eight key competences for lifelong learning that the council confirmed in its recommendation adopted earlier this year. member states are also working to embed digital technologies in the school environment. investment in resources and infrastructure is crucial but, if it is to lead to innovation, it needs to be matched with a comprehensive vision for digital transformation that tackles the educational organisation as a whole. the commission thinks that selfie, a free online tool just launched in october, can play a vital role here: it can help any school assess its use of technologies for teaching and learning. by the end of november, over 50 000 teachers, students and school leaders had already used selfie. our goal is to reach one million users by the end of next year. i also welcome the emphasis on teachers and trainers in your report. i fully agree that teaching staff are at the heart of digital transformation. teachers do of course need training, but crucially they need time and support to be able to use technology in their teaching. technology should also help relieve teachers from the burden of administrative and non-teaching tasks. moreover, in its proposal for the next multiannual financial framework (mff), the commission has demonstrated commitment to supporting educators and learners in gaining and updating skills and competencies, including the digital ones. the eu budget support for digital skills development will not only be guaranteed by the successes of the existing eu funding instruments covering this field, such as esf+, erasmus and horizon europe, but also by the new digital europe programme. the digital europe programme will provide learning opportunities reflecting the latest technological developments in advanced digital skills relating to artificial intelligence, high—performance computing and cybersecurity. as you rightly say in your report, all schools need to be provided with high capacity broadband. in fact, within the future connecting europe facility (cef) programme, after 2020 we aim to support access to gigabit connectivity for socioeconomic drivers, including schools. with the future digital europe programme, in turn, we plan to support the deployment of digital capabilities in schools, and the future erasmus programme will support schools, universities and other educational organisations in their digital transformation. we therefore strongly welcome your support in the negotiation of these new programmes. i fully agree with the honourable members that the action plan can only be the starting point of a broader debate. in keeping with the wishes of the member states in gothenburg, we deliberately started with schools, higher education and vocational education and training. adult learning, non-formal education and teacher training will follow at the next stage. the plan has helped a great deal in generating political discussion and momentum, and we intend to build on this. to conclude, i would like to thank you again for this constructive discussion. the commission looks forward to working with you in developing a fuller vision and strategy.
the rapporteur. madam president, i would first like to thank the shadow rapporteurs with whom i have had a very good working relationship. we have therefore come to a text which i consider to be very important for european culture. it is a good coincidence that this text is being presented at the end of the european year of cultural heritage, which has highlighted the brilliance and importance of european culture. in drafting this report, i had in mind the many challenges facing europe today: the economic crisis, the refugee problem, the rise of populism and the far right. in these difficult times, civilization can lead to the rediscovery of the common bonds that unite and bind us. in this context, we have worked in the committee on culture and have come up with specific proposals, which revolve around three general themes: first, adequate funding for culture. the creative europe programme needs to be at least doubled. it is also necessary to establish strong partnerships with the european structural and investment funds so that actions for culture reach 1% of the next budget. the truth, madam president, which was pointed out by the great politician and orator of ancient greece, demosthenes, 'without money, no man is born', is as relevant as ever. secondly, the protection of the rights of authors. the nature of the work of creators is known to often lead to economic uncertainty, and they are often exposed to the interests of multinational corporations. the adoption of the copyright directive last september was an important step in the right direction. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. thirdly, the mobility of creators and their works. the european union must make a decisive contribution to the mobility of creators, in order to facilitate their contacts and enable them to reach the widest possible audience, both within the union and beyond. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very important one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. it is the creation of an institution which, in the spirit of the european capital of culture, will each year highlight a personality whose work and life have expressed and influenced european values. i could give you random but specific examples, such as mozart, leonardo da vinci, shakespeare, plato, goethe, marie curie, but also simone de beauvoir, cervantes, and so many others. culture is expressed and flourishes by people, and it is time their contribution was recognised at a pan-european level. in conclusion, madam president, i would like to stress that the adoption of the report will, for the first time in many years, contribute to a coherent and coordinated cultural path for the member states. it will be the best follow-up to the european year of cultural heritage, which is leaving behind its strong sparkle and legacy.

the digital economy is really the future of europe, madam president, because it can boost competitiveness. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. fortunately, they've realized lately that they need to invest in the digital economy, in new technologies. but, in order to make an investment, there must be a comfort in public investment. but how can there be public investment when the stability pact creates certain problems and limits the member states themselves in the ability to allocate funds? the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the digital economy is not a one-off, but an exceptional one, and that it does not count towards the deficit, when there is investment in the digital economy, and especially when it is an investment in order to create opportunities for the digital economy to grow. at the same time, we have to look at it from the perspective of a central bank, because mr draghi has bought bonds from companies that have not invested in the digital economy. it is simply a matter of looking at it, because the funds invested, the funds you mention in the programme, are not enough, so we have to find other ways of financing.
mr president, it has been reported by colleagues that there is a real problem on the border between bosnia and croatia. you said there were five and a half thousand refugees crowded in. so imagine the problem on the island of samos, where there are currently six thousand refugees and illegal immigrants on one island alone. but i didn't hear any suggestions. i didn't hear any solutions being offered. just wishes. question number one: how have these thousands of refugees and illegal immigrants arrived in bosnia? in what way? have they crossed into other countries? you said they came from turkey. but how did they get to bosnia? is there a flight connection to turkish airlines? who has the responsibility of getting these people there? that's what we're looking for. secondly, there are certainly obligations for bosnia, because it has an association agreement with the european union. as for croatia, a colleague said that croatia should join schengen. i tell you that if croatia joins schengen, you will be a magnet for more refugees and illegal immigrants, as is the case with greece.
mr. chairman, i want to join my colleagues who have clearly described the situation in tanzania. the government of president magufuli is a big disappointment for us, we expected after his election that he would make real reforms in the country, that he would fight corruption, and instead we are witnessing the hunt for members of sexual minorities, pregnant girls, opposition leaders and so on. that means that the situation is going in a very bad direction, and i have a question for the commissioner: how will the commission react to this in particular? i think it is necessary that the developments in tanzania have an impact on the relations between the european union and tanzania and that we do not stop at mere proclamations, political and moral condemnation of the situation in tanzania, but that we translate this political position into action. this means that it should have an impact on the economic aid to tanzania from the european union and, say, on the tightening of diplomatic and public pressure on the country.
mr. president, the situation in tanzania is out of control. we see that president magufuli is now persecuting the opposition, civil society groups, parties with which he does not agree. he's taken out radio stations and newspapers. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat this problem, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for his report. we have a ban on pregnant women and girls from school, which is unacceptable. we also see that in the maasai land area there is now land grabbing. land that is arable is taken for profit. it is obvious that the european union should take very concrete measures against the tanzanian government.
